Q1. Three workers receive ₹9000 for a job. Their contributions are proportional to 30:30:12. How much will C get?
A. ₹1500
B. ₹1505
C. ₹1498
D. ₹1800
Answer: ₹1500
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_wage_with_helper
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
Share=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Use contribution ratio for the share.
\[
Share=\frac{12}{72}\times 9000
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
Share=1500
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
Share=contribution\ ratio\times wage
\]
Answer: ₹1500

---

Q2. A job is finished by 10 workers in 15 days when each works 8 hours daily. How many workers are needed for 20 days at 6 hours daily?
A. 14 workers
B. 10 workers
C. 13 workers
D. 15 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
10\times 15\times 8=M_2\times 20\times 6
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{10\times 15\times 8}{20\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

Q3. Given the working capacity ratios A:B = 3:5 and B:C = 7:8, what is the ratio A:B:C?
A. 3:5:8
B. 21:35:40
C. 40:35:21
D. 3:7:8
Answer: 21:35:40
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_chain
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
B_c
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
LCM(5,7)=35
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
A:B:C=21:35:40
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A:B:C=21:35:40
\]
Answer: 21:35:40

---

Q4. A team repairs 15 metres daily, but another effect damages 5 metres daily. How many days are needed to complete 180 metres?
A. 17 days
B. 18 days
C. 21 3/5 days
D. 14 days
Answer: 18 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_negative_positive_mixed_work
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T=\frac{180}{15-5}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 18 days

---

Q5. A printer prints 100 pages per hour for 5 hours. If only 90% of the pages are accepted, how many pages are accepted?
A. 540 pages
B. 450 pages
C. 451 pages
D. 455 pages
Answer: 450 pages
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_work_quality_rejection
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
O_a=O\times \frac{p}{100}
\]
Use contribution ratio for the share.
\[
O_a=100\times 5\times \frac{90}{100}
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
O_a=450
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O_a=gross\times p/100
\]
Answer: 450 pages

Q6. A packing job has 300 boxes. If 2 men and 3 women work together, with each man doing 4 boxes and each woman 2 boxes daily, how many days will it take?
A. 20.83 days
B. 13 2/3 days
C. 11 2/3 days
D. 16 2/3 days
Answer: 16 2/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_equivalence_men_women_children
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T=\frac{300}{2\times 4+3\times 2+4\times 1}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=16\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 16 2/3 days

---

Q7. A group is expected to complete a work in 15 days. It has already worked for 4 days. What fraction remains?
A. 11/4
B. 11/15
C. 4/15
D. 15/11
Answer: 11/15
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
R=1-\frac{4}{15}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
R=\frac{11}{15}=11/15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{15-4}{15}=11/15
\]
Answer: 11/15

---

Q8. A tap and a drain work alternately for one hour each. The tap adds 12 litres and the drain removes 5 litres. How many hours are needed for a 60-litre tank?
A. 20 1/3 hours
B. 13.07 hours
C. 14 1/3 hours
D. 16 1/3 hours
Answer: 16 1/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_alternating_spill_cycle
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
W_{cycle}=12-5
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
60\ units,\ cycle=7
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=16\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=C_f+t_f
\]
Answer: 16 1/3 hours

---

\]

Shortcut / Exam Method:

Q10. There are 720 ration packets in a camp. If 36 packets are used each day, how many days will they last?
A. 26 days
B. 22 days
C. 20 days
D. 16 days
Answer: 20 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_resource_rate_equivalence
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720}{36}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Answer: 20 days

---

Q11. A team completes 3 files per day for 2 days and then 2 files per day after a team change. How many days are required for 12 files?
A. 4 days
B. 1/2 days
C. 6 1/4 days
D. 5 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_replacement_worker
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
r_1t_1+r_2t_2=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=3\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{6}{2}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 5 days

---

Q12. Two workers take turns, with A starting. A handles 6 files per turn and B handles 6 files per turn. In how many hours will 72 files be finished?
A. 14 2/5 hours
B. 12 hours
C. 13 hours
D. 17 hours
Answer: 12 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_hours
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=6+6
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 hours

---

Q14. A does \(\frac{4}{9}\) of a job in 9 days. What is the time required for the complete job?

C. 4 days
D. 81 days
Answer: 20 1/4 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_partial_completion_then_team
Explanation:

\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T=9\times \frac{9}{4}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=20.25
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=9\div \frac{4}{9}=20.25
\]
Answer: 20 1/4 days

Q15. If wages for equal time are ₹450 and ₹600 for A and B, what is their work ratio?
A. 45:30
B. 600:450
C. 1050:600
D. 3:4
Answer: 3:4
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_efficiency_from_wages
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
W_A:W_B=E_A:E_B
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
E_A:E_B=450:600
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
E_A:E_B=3:4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=3:4
\]
Answer: 3:4

Q16. A, B and C together take 8 days. A and B together take 16 days. How long will C take alone?
A. 37 days
B. 38 2/5 days
C. 32 days
D. 33 days
Answer: 32 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_unknown_worker_from_team
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{16}-\frac{1}{32}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_X=32
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 32 days

---

Q17. A work requires 10 workers for 15 days with 8 working hours per day. How many workers will be needed for 20 days with 6 hours per day?
A. 14 workers
B. 10 workers
C. 13 workers
D. 15 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_road_construction_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
10\times 15\times 8=M_2\times 20\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{10\times 15\times 8}{20\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

\[
W_{cycle}=12-5
\]

\[
60\ units,\ cycle=7
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=16\frac{1}{3}

Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=C_f+t_f
\]
Answer: 16 1/3 hours

Q20. The working capacity of A is to B as 5:7. If both finish the job in 8 days, how many days will A take alone?
A. 5.71 days
B. 20 days
C. 19 1/5 days
D. 11 1/5 days
Answer: 19 1/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_relative_efficiency_percent
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
W=(5+7)\times 8
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_A=\frac{96}{5}=19.2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(5+7)8}{5}=19.2
\]
Answer: 19 1/5 days

Q22. A job is completed by A, B and C together in 6 days. A and B together can do it in 20 days. What is C's individual time?
A. 8.57 days
B. 12.57 days
C. 7.57 days
D. 12.86 days
Answer: 8.57 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_pairwise_total_abc
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{6}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_X=8.57
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 8.57 days

Q23. A alone finishes a job in 20 days, while B alone finishes it in 30 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?
A. 50 days
B. 12 days
C. 25 days
D. 20 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(20,30)=60
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=60/20=3,\quad B=60/30=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{3+2}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{20\times 30}{20+30}=12
\]
Answer: 12 days

---

Q24. A machine packs 15 boxes per day for 6 days and then packs 12 boxes per day. In how many days will 180 boxes be packed?
A. 10 1/2 days
B. 13 1/2 days
C. 8 1/2 days
D. 16.88 days
Answer: 13 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_productivity_decay
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-r_1t_1}{r_2}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T=6+\frac{180-15\times 6}{12}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=13.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+t_2
\]
Answer: 13 1/2 days

Q27. Two filling pipes and one emptying pipe are opened together. Their times are 12, 18 and 36 hours respectively. What is the time to fill the tank?
A. 11 1/4 hours
B. 11 hours
C. 9 hours
D. 4 hours
Answer: 9 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_two_fillers_one_empty
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_f-r_e}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12+1/18-1/36
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 9 hours

Q28. A can do a work in 20 days. With B, the same work is completed in 12 days. Find B's time in days?
A. 8 days
B. 32 days
C. 16 days
D. 30 days
Answer: 30 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_individual_from_combined
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
B=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{12\times 20}{20-12}=30
\]
Answer: 30 days

Q32. 12 workers were expected to complete a job in the planned time. After working for 10 days, 2 workers left. By how many days will the job be delayed?
A. 4 days
B. 4 4/5 days
C. 9 days
D. 2 days
Answer: 4 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_worker_removed_delay
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
D=T_{actual}-T_{planned}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
R=360-12\times 10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
D=\frac{240}{10}-\frac{240}{12}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=T_a-T_p
\]
Answer: 4 days

Q33. Three machines work in parallel and make 12, 15 and 18 parts per hour. For 6 operating hours respectively, what is the total number of parts?

\[
O=12\times 6+15\times 6+18\times 6
\]

\[
O=270
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=(r_1+r_2+r_3)t
\]
Answer: 270 items

Q34. 15 workers working 6 hours per day finish the job in 16 days. If the daily working time is 8 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days?
A. 20 workers
B. 15 workers
C. 11 workers
D. 18 workers
Answer: 15 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_painting_walls_area_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
15\times 16\times 6=M_2\times 12\times 8
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{15\times 16\times 6}{12\times 8}=15
\]
Answer: 15 workers

Q35. 16 farm workers can harvest a field in 12 days by working 6 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 18 days by working 8 hours a day?

Q36. 8 workers have done 64 files out of 240. To finish the rest in 8 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 18 workers
B. 15 workers
C. 11.2 workers
D. 14 workers
Answer: 14 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{176}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 14 workers

Q38. 64 of 300 forms are complete. 8 workers are available and 8 days remain. How many additional workers are required?
A. 26.4 workers

Answer: 22 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{236}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=22
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 22 workers

Q40. A tank has two filling pipes. Pipe A fills it in 12 hours and pipe B in 18 hours. pipe A is closed after 2 hours. What is the total filling time?

Q41. A works on day 1, rests on day 2, and repeats this pattern. A completes 10 metres on a working day. In how many days will 120 metres be completed?

D. 20 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_work_rest_cycle
Explanation:

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]

\[
W_{cycle}=10+0
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 24 days

Q44. One worker can do a job in 20 days and another can do it in 8 days. How long will they take together?
A. 28 days
B. 5.71 days
C. 14 days
D. 8 days
Answer: 5.71 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(20,8)=40
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=40/20=2,\quad B=40/8=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{40}{2+5}=5.71
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{20\times 8}{20+8}=5.71
\]
Answer: 5.71 days

Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_worker_removed_delay
Explanation:

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Q86. One worker can do a job in 18 days and another can do it in 6 days. How long will they take together?

Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work

\[

\]

\[

\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{18}{1+3}=4.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{18\times 6}{18+6}=4.5
\]
Answer: 4 1/2 days

\]

15\times 16\times 6=M_2\times 12\times 8
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
M_2=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{15\times 16\times 6}{12\times 8}=15
\]
Answer: 15 workers

Q120. A work order has 210 forms. The first team completes 49 forms per day for 3 days, then the changed team completes 45 per day. What is the completion time?

D. 5 2/5 days
Answer: 4 2/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_replacement_worker
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
r_1t_1+r_2t_2=W
\]

\[
W_1=49\times 3
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[

\]

\[

\]
Answer: 4 2/5 days

Q121. A and B work by turns. A starts and completes 8 documents per turn; B completes 5. How many hours will 60 documents take?

Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_hours
Explanation:

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]

\[
W_{cycle}=8+5
\]

\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 9 hours

A. 13 workers

C. 27 workers
D. 10 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_road_construction_work
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q131. If A can complete a work in 30 days and B can complete it in 36 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?
A. 16.36 days
B. 30 days
C. 33 days
D. 66 days
Answer: 16.36 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(30,36)=180
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=180/30=6,\quad B=180/36=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{180}{6+5}=16.36
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{30\times 36}{30+36}=16.36
\]
Answer: 16.36 days

\]

\]
Answer: 5.54 days

Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[

\]

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]
Answer: 23.85 days

Q189. A can complete a work in 30 days and B can complete it in 12 days. A starts alone, and after 3 days B joins him. In how many days from the start will the whole work be completed?
A. 8.71 days
B. 8.57 days
C. 10.71 days
D. 14.71 days
Answer: 10.71 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=6\times 3,\quad R=180-18
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{162}{6+15}=10.71
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{180-6\times 3}{6+15}
\]
Answer: 10.71 days

\]

\[

Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 912 pages

Q225. If a work is completed in 12 days and 3 days have passed, what fraction of the work is yet to be done?

\]

\]

Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=52\times 4,\quad R=780-208
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{572}{52+39}=10.29
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{780-52\times 4}{52+39}
\]
Answer: 10.29 days

Q263. 18 workers can complete a job in 8 days by working 5 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days by working 6 hours a day?

Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic

\[

\[

\[

\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q299. 8 equal workers begin a job. After 10 days, only 5 workers remain. By how many days is the work delayed?

R=300-8\times 10
\]

\[

\]

Q300. A team of 8 workers has completed 48 items from a 240-item order. With 12 days left, how many workers must be added?
A. 8 workers
B. 10 workers

D. 14 workers
Answer: 8 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{192}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 8 workers

Q304. A can finish a work in 18 days and B in 15 days. They work together for 5 days, then B alone finishes it. How many days are taken altogether?

Q305. If A can complete a work in 6 days and B can complete it in 30 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?
A. 5 days
B. 36 days
C. 6 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(6,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/6=5,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{5+1}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 30}{6+30}=5
\]
Answer: 5 days

Q307. A alone takes 12 days and B alone takes 18 days. During a joint job, A does not work in the last 2 days and B does not work in the last 3 days. What is the total time?
A. 12 days
B. 8 3/5 days
C. 4 3/5 days
D. 9 3/5 days
Answer: 9 3/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
6(T-2)+4(T-3)=72
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=9.6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 9 3/5 days

Q336. A works alone for 3 days on a job. A's time is 6 days and B's time is 15 days. How many days are needed from the start if B joins afterward?

Answer: 5.14 days

\[

\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{105}{35+14}=5.14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{210-35\times 3}{35+14}
\]
Answer: 5.14 days

\]

Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 6 workers

Q373. A works every alternate day and rests on the next day. On each working day A lays 8 metres of cable. If 72 metres are to be laid, in how many days will the work be completed?

C. 22 days
D. 21 days
Answer: 18 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_work_rest_cycle
Explanation:

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+0
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 18 days

Q374. A and B begin a job together. Their individual times are 18 days and 24 days. A leaves after 4 days. What is the total completion time?
A. 24 2/3 days
B. 18 2/3 days
C. 15 2/3 days
D. 14.93 days
Answer: 18 2/3 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(20+15)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{220}{15}=18\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 18 2/3 days

Q375. One worker can do a job in 20 days and another can do it in 26 days. How long will they take together?
A. 23 days
B. 11.3 days
C. 46 days
D. 20 days
Answer: 11.3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(20,26)=260
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=260/20=13,\quad B=260/26=10
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{260}{13+10}=11.3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{20\times 26}{20+26}=11.3
\]
Answer: 11.3 days

Q411. A pipe can fill a tank in 15 hours. Due to a leak, the tank is filled in 21 hours. In how many hours can the leak empty the full tank?

B. 50 1/2 hours
C. 63 hours
D. 57 1/2 hours
Answer: 52 1/2 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{21}
\]

\[
L=52.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 52 1/2 hours

Q426. If A can complete a work in 24 days and B can complete it in 30 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?
A. 27 days
B. 24 days
C. 54 days
D. 13 1/3 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(24,30)=120
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=120/24=5,\quad B=120/30=4
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{120}{5+4}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{24\times 30}{24+30}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{30}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
B=10.91
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{8\times 30}{30-8}=10.91
\]
Answer: 10.91 days

Q453. A and B can separately complete a job in 30 days and 8 days. What is the time taken when they work together?

Answer: 6.32 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:

\[
W=LCM(30,8)=120
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=120/30=4,\quad B=120/8=15
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{120}{4+15}=6.32
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{30\times 8}{30+8}=6.32
\]
Answer: 6.32 days

Put the given values into that relation.
\[
R=1-\frac{4}{10}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
R=\frac{6}{10}=3/5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{10-4}{10}=3/5
\]
Answer: 3/5

---

Q486. 20 people have food for 24 days. If the number of people becomes 40, for how many days will the food last?
A. 8 days
B. 12 days
C. 13 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
20\times 24=40\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 24}{40}
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q487. A job has 60 files. The team handles 10 files daily at first for 3 days, and then 19 files daily. What is the total time?

B. 5.58 days
C. 4.58 days
D. 5.72 days
Answer: 4.58 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_multi_phase_join_leave
Explanation:

\[
\sum r_it_i=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=10\times 3
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{30}{19}=4.58
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 4.58 days

Q490. Workers build 12 metres of boundary each day, while 5 metres are spoiled daily. In how many days will 120 metres be completed?

\[
T=\frac{120}{12-5}

T=17.14

\]

Q491. A needs 20 days for a work and A with B needs 8 days. In how many days can B do the work alone?

\[

\]

\[

\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

Q492. 12 workers have completed 96 of 360 forms. Only 8 days are left. How many extra workers are needed to finish on time?

\[

\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{264}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=21
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 21 workers

Q495. A and B begin a job together. Their individual times are 30 days and 8 days. A leaves after 4 days. What is the total completion time?
A. 5.93 days
B. 6.93 days
C. 8 2/3 days
D. 1.93 days
Answer: 6.93 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(28+105)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{308}{105}=6.93
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 6.93 days

Q496. If A can complete a work in 15 days and B can complete it in 30 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?
A. 10 days
B. 45 days
C. 15 days
D. 22 1/2 days
Answer: 10 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(15,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/15=2,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{2+1}=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{15\times 30}{15+30}=10
\]
Answer: 10 days

Q498. A job needs 6 days of work. Work stops for 1 days during the job. How many days pass from start to finish?

Answer: 7 days

Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[
T=T_{active}+1=7
\]

\[

\]

Q500. For a fixed time, what is the ratio of work done by A and B if their individual times are 15 days and 21 days?
A. 36:21
B. 21:36
C. 7:5
D. 15:21
Answer: 7:5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_done_ratio_from_times
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{15}:\frac{1}{21}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
W_A:W_B=21:15
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
W_A:W_B=7:5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=7:5
\]
Answer: 7:5
